\|{\bm{V}}\|_{F}=\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}
and
\bm{W}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,\bar{R}_{\mu}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
, there exist dataset dependent constants
C,c>0
such that

\displaystyle C\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-\bm{s}_{i\alpha_{i}}\right)\geq-\Big\langle\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}),\bm{V}\Big\rangle\geq c\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-\bm{s}_{i\alpha_{i}}\right)>0,

(62a)

\displaystyle\|{\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})}\|_{F}\leq\bar{A}C\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-\bm{s}_{i\alpha_{i}}\right),

(62b)

\displaystyle-\left<\frac{\bm{V}}{\|{\bm{V}}\|_{F}},\frac{\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})}{\|{\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})}\|_{F}}\right>\geq\frac{c}{C}\cdot\frac{\Theta}{\bar{A}}>0.

(62c)

Here,
\bm{s}_{i\alpha_{i}}=(\mathbb{S}({\bm{X}}_{i}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}_{i}))_{\alpha_{i}}
,
\bar{A}=\max_{i\in[n],t,\tau\in[T]}\|{(\bm{x}_{it}-\bm{x}_{i\tau})}\|~\|{{\bm{z}}_{i}}\|
, and
\Theta=1/\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}
.

Proof.

Let
R=\bar{R}_{\mu}
,
(\mathcal{T}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
be the set of all
support indices
per Definition
2
. Let
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{i}=[T]-\mathcal{T}_{i}-\{\alpha_{i}\}
be the non-
support indices
. Let

\begin{split}&\Theta=1/\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F},\\
&\delta=\frac{1}{2}\min_{i\in[n]}\min_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i},\tau\in\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{i}}(\bm{x}_{it}-\bm{x}_{i\tau})^{\top}\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}{\bm{z}}_{i},\\
&A=\max_{i\in[n],t\in[T]}\frac{\|{\bm{x}_{it}{\bm{z}}_{i}^{\top}}\|_{F}}{\Theta},\\
&\mu\leq\mu(\delta)=\frac{1}{8}\left(\frac{\min(0.5,\delta)}{A}\right)^{2}.\end{split}

(63)

Since
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}
is the max-margin model ensuring
(\bm{x}_{i\alpha_{i}}-\bm{x}_{it})^{\top}\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}{\bm{z}}_{i}\geq 1
, the following inequalities hold for all
\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mu}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}),~\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}=\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}
and all
i\in[n],t\in\mathcal{T}_{i},\tau\in\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{i}
:

\begin{split}(\bm{x}_{it}-\bm{x}_{i\tau})^{\top}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}_{i}&\geq\delta>0,\\
(\bm{x}_{i\alpha_{i}}-\bm{x}_{i\tau})^{\top}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}_{i}&\geq 1+\delta,\\
\frac{3}{2}\geq(\bm{x}_{i\alpha_{i}}-\bm{x}_{it})^{\top}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}_{i}&\geq\frac{1}{2}.\end{split}

(64)

Here, we used
\|{\bm{W}-\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}^{2}/\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 2\mu
which implies
\|{\bm{W}-\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{2\mu}/\Theta
.

To proceed, we write the gradient correlation following (
15
) and (
27
)

\displaystyle\left<\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}),\bm{V}\right>

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell^{\prime}_{i}\cdot\bm{h}_{i}^{\top}\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\tilde{\bm{h}}_{i})\bm{\gamma}_{i},

(65)

where we denoted
\ell^{\prime}_{i}=\ell^{\prime}(Y_{i}\cdot\bm{v}^{\top}{\bm{X}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbb{S}(\tilde{\bm{h}}_{i}))
,
\bm{h}_{i}={\bm{X}}_{i}\bm{V}{\bm{z}}_{i}
,
\tilde{\bm{h}}_{i}={\bm{X}}_{i}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}_{i}
, and
\bm{s}_{i}=\mathbb{S}(\tilde{\bm{h}}_{i})
.

Using (
64
), for all
t\in\mathcal{T}_{i},\tau\in\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{i}
, for all
\bm{W}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
, we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{h}}_{it}-\tilde{\bm{h}}_{i\tau}\geq R\Theta\delta,

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{h}}_{i\alpha_{i}}-\tilde{\bm{h}}_{i\tau}\geq R\Theta(1+\delta),

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{h}}_{i\alpha_{i}}-\tilde{\bm{h}}_{it}\geq R\Theta/2.

Consequently, we can bound the softmax probabilities
\bm{s}_{i}=\mathbb{S}(\tilde{\bm{h}}_{i})
over non-
support indices
as follows: For all
i\in[n]
and any
t_{i}\in\mathcal{T}_{i}

\displaystyle S_{i}:=\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{i\tau}\leq Te^{-R\Theta/2}\bm{s}_{i\alpha_{i}}\leq Te^{-R\Theta/2},

(66a)

\displaystyle Q_{i}:=\sum_{\tau\in\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{i}}\bm{s}_{i\tau}\leq Te^{-R\Theta\delta}\bm{s}_{it_{i}}\leq Te^{-R\Theta\delta}S_{i}.

(66b)

Recall scores
\bm{\gamma}_{it}=Y_{i}\cdot\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{x}_{it}
. Define the score gaps over
support indices
:

\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{i}=\bm{\gamma}_{i\alpha_{i}}-\max_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{\gamma}_{it}~~~\textnormal{and}~~~\bar{\gamma}^{\textsl{gap}}_{i}=\bm{\gamma}_{i\alpha_{i}}-\min_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{\gamma}_{it}.

It follows from (
63
) that

\displaystyle A=\max_{i\in[n],t\in[T]}\frac{\|{\bm{x}_{it}{\bm{z}}_{i}^{\top}}\|_{F}}{\Theta}\geq\max_{i\in[n],t\in[T]}\|{\bm{h}_{it}}\|.

Define the
{\bm{\alpha}}
-dependent global scalar
\Gamma=\sup_{i\in[n],t,\tau\in[T]}|\bm{\gamma}_{it}-\bm{\gamma}_{i\tau}|
.

Let us focus on a fixed datapoint
i\in[n]
, assume (without losing generality)
\alpha_{i}=1
, and drop subscripts
i
.
Directly applying Lemma
8
, we obtain

\big|\bm{h}^{\top}\text{diag}(\bm{s})\bm{\gamma}-\bm{h}^{\top}\bm{s}\bm{s}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}-\sum_{t\geq 2}^{T}(\bm{h}_{1}-\bm{h}_{t})\bm{s}_{t}(\bm{\gamma}_{1}-\bm{\gamma}_{t})\big|\leq 2\Gamma A(1-\bm{s}_{1})^{2}.

To proceed, let us decouple the non-
support indices
within
\sum_{t\geq 2}^{T}(\bm{h}_{1}-\bm{h}_{t})\bm{s}_{t}(\bm{\gamma}_{1}-\bm{\gamma}_{t})
via

\big|\sum_{t\in\bar{\mathcal{T}}}(\bm{h}_{1}-\bm{h}_{t})\bm{s}_{t}(\bm{\gamma}_{1}-\bm{\gamma}_{t})\big|\leq 2Q\Gamma A.

Aggregating these, we found

\displaystyle\big|\bm{h}^{\top}\text{diag}(\bm{s})\bm{\gamma}-\bm{h}^{\top}\bm{s}\bm{s}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}-\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}(\bm{h}_{1}-\bm{h}_{t})\bm{s}_{t}(\bm{\gamma}_{1}-\bm{\gamma}_{t})\big|\leq 2\Gamma A((1-\bm{s}_{1})^{2}+Q).

(67)

To proceed, let us upper/lower bound the gradient correlation. We use two bounds depending on
\bm{V}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mu}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
(
Case 1
) or general
\bm{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
(
Case 2
).

\bullet

Case 1:
\bm{V}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mu}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
.
Since
1.5\geq\bm{h}_{1}-\bm{h}_{t}\geq 0.5
following (
64
), we find

1.5\cdot S\cdot\bar{\gamma}^{\textsl{gap}}\geq\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}(\bm{h}_{1}-\bm{h}_{t})\bm{s}_{t}(\bm{\gamma}_{1}-\bm{\gamma}_{t})\geq 0.5\cdot S\cdot\gamma^{\textsl{gap}},

where recall the definition of
S
(having dropped subscripts) in (
66a
).

\bullet

Case 2:
{\bm{V}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
\|{\bm{V}}\|_{F}=\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}
.
Define
\bar{A}=\max_{i\in[n],t,\tau\in[T]}\|{\bm{x}_{it}-\bm{x}_{i\tau}}\|~\|{{\bm{z}}_{i}}\|
. For any
\|{\bm{V}}\|_{F}=\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|
, we use the fact that

\|{\bm{h}_{1}-\bm{h}_{t}}\|\leq\|{(\bm{x}_{it}-\bm{x}_{i\tau}){\bm{z}}_{i}^{\top}}\|_{F}\cdot\|{\bm{V}}\|_{F}\leq\frac{\bar{A}}{\Theta}.

Note that by definition
\frac{\bar{A}}{\Theta}\geq 1
. To proceed, we can upper bound

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{A}}{\Theta}\cdot S\cdot\bar{\gamma}^{\textsl{gap}}\geq\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}}(\bm{h}_{1}-\bm{h}_{t})\bm{s}_{t}(\bm{\gamma}_{1}-\bm{\gamma}_{t}).

(68)

Next we claim that for both cases,
S
dominates
((1-\bm{s}_{1})^{2}+Q)
for large
R
. Specifically, we wish for

\displaystyle\frac{S\cdot\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}}{4}\geq 4\Gamma A\max((1-\bm{s}_{1})^{2},Q)\iff S\geq 16\frac{\Gamma A}{\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}}\max((1-\bm{s}_{1})^{2},Q).

(69)

Now choose
R\geq\delta^{-1}\log(T)/\Theta
to ensure
Q\leq S
since
Q\leq Te^{-R\Theta\delta}S
from (
66a
). Consequently

(1-\bm{s}_{1})^{2}=(Q+S)^{2}\leq 4S^{2}\leq 4STe^{-R\Theta/2}.

Combining these, what we wish is ensured by guaranteeing

\displaystyle S\geq 16\frac{\Gamma A}{\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}}\max(4STe^{-R\Theta/2},Te^{-R\Theta\delta}S).

(70)

This in turn is ensured for all inputs
i\in[n]
by choosing

\displaystyle R\geq\frac{\max(2,\delta^{-1})}{\Theta}\log\left(\frac{64T\Gamma A}{\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}}\right),

(71)

where
\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}=\min_{i\in[n]}\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{i}
is the global scalar which is the worst case score gap over all inputs.

\bullet

Case 1:
\bm{V}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mu}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
. With the above choice of
R
, we guaranteed

2(1-\bm{s}_{1})\cdot\bar{\gamma}^{\textsl{gap}}\geq 2\cdot S\cdot\bar{\gamma}^{\textsl{gap}}\geq\bm{h}^{\top}\text{diag}(\bm{s})\bm{\gamma}-\bm{h}^{\top}\bm{s}\bm{s}^{\top}\bm{\gamma}\geq\frac{S\cdot\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}}{4}\geq\frac{(1-\bm{s}_{1})\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}}{8}.

via (
69
) and (
67
).

Since this holds over all inputs, going back to the gradient correlation (
65
) and averaging above over all inputs
i\in[n]
and plugging back the indices
i
, we obtain the advertised bound

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}-\ell^{\prime}_{i}\cdot S_{i}\cdot\bar{\gamma}^{\textsl{gap}}_{i}\geq-\left<\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}),\bm{V}\right>\geq\frac{1}{8n}\sum_{i\in[n]}-\ell^{\prime}_{i}\cdot S_{i}\cdot\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{i}.

(72)

Let
-\ell^{\prime}_{\min/\max}
be the min/max values negative loss derivative admits over the ball
[-A,A]
and note that
\max_{i\in[n]}\bar{\gamma}^{\textsl{gap}}_{i}>0
and
\min_{i\in[n]}\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{i}>0
are dataset dependent constants. Then, we declare the constants
C=-2\ell^{\prime}_{\max}\cdot\max_{i\in[n]}\bar{\gamma}^{\textsl{gap}}_{i}>0,c=-(1/8)\ell^{\prime}_{\min}\cdot\min_{i\in[n]}\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{i}>0
to obtain the bound (
62a
).

\bullet

Case 2:
{\bm{V}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
\|{\bm{V}}\|_{F}=\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}
.
Next, we show (
62b
) and (
62c
). For any
\bm{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
satisfying
\|{\bm{V}}\|_{F}=\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}
, using (
68
) and the choice of
R
in (
71
) similarly guarantees

\frac{2\bar{A}}{\Theta}(1-\bm{s}_{1})\bar{\gamma}^{\textsl{gap}}\geq\bm{h}^{\top}\text{diag}(\bm{s})\bm{\gamma}-\bm{h}^{\top}\bm{s}\bm{s}^{\top}\bm{\gamma},

for fixed input. Going back to the gradient correlation (
65
) and averaging above over all inputs
i\in[n]
, with the same definition of
C>0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{A}C}{\Theta n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(1-\bm{s}_{i\alpha_{i}})\geq-\left<\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}),\bm{V}\right>.

(73)

To proceed, since (
73
) holds for any
\bm{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, we observe that when setting
\bm{V}=\frac{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}{\|{\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})}\|_{F}}\cdot\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})
, this implies that

\left<\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}),\bm{V}\right>=\|{\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})}\|_{F}\cdot\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}\leq\frac{\bar{A}C}{\Theta n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(1-\bm{s}_{i\alpha_{i}}).

Simplifying
\Theta=1/\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}
on both sides gives (
62b
).

Combining the above inequality with (
72
), we obtain that for all
\bm{V},\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mu}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})

-\left<\frac{\bm{V}}{\|{\bm{V}}\|_{F}},\frac{\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})}{\|{\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})}\|_{F}}\right>\geq\frac{c\Theta}{C\bar{A}},

which gives (
62c
).

Lemma 14

Suppose Assumption
A
on the loss function
\ell
holds, and let
{\bm{\alpha}}=(\alpha_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
be locally optimal tokens according to Definition
2
. Let
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}=\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}
denote the SVM solution obtained via (
Att-SVM
) by replacing
(\texttt{opt}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
with
\bm{\alpha}=(\alpha_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
. Let
\mu=\mu({\bm{\alpha}})>0
and
\bar{R}_{\mu}
be defined as in Lemma
13
. For any choice of
\pi>0
, there exists
R_{\pi}\geq\bar{R}_{\mu}
such that, for any
\bm{W}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R_{\pi}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
, we have

\left<\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}),\frac{\bm{W}}{\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}}\right>\geq(1+\pi)\left<\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}),\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right>.

Proof.

Let
R=R_{\pi}
,
{\bm{\bar{W}}}=\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}\bm{W}/\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}
,
\bm{h}_{i}={\bm{X}}_{i}{\bm{\bar{W}}}{\bm{z}}_{i}
, and
\bm{\bar{h}}_{i}={\bm{X}}_{i}\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}{\bm{z}}_{i}
. To establish the result, we will prove that, for sufficiently large
R
and for any
\bm{W}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
:

\displaystyle\left<-\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}),\frac{\bm{W}}{\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}}\right>

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell^{\prime}_{i}\cdot\left<\bm{h}_{i},\mathbb{S}^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{i}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}_{i})\bm{\gamma}_{i}\right>

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1+\pi}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell^{\prime}_{i}\cdot\left<\bm{\bar{h}}_{i},\mathbb{S}^{\prime}({\bm{X}}_{i}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}_{i})\bm{\gamma}_{i}\right>=(1+\pi)\left<-\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}),\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right>.

(74)

Following (
67
), for all
\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mu}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
with
\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}=\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}
,
\tilde{\bm{h}}={\bm{X}}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}
, and
\bm{s}=\mathbb{S}(\tilde{\bm{h}})
, we have found

\displaystyle\big|\tilde{\bm{h}}^{\top}_{i}\text{diag}(\bm{s}_{i})\bm{\gamma}_{i}-\tilde{\bm{h}}^{\top}_{i}\bm{s}_{i}\bm{s}^{\top}_{i}\bm{\gamma}_{i}-\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}(\tilde{\bm{h}}_{i1}-\tilde{\bm{h}}_{it})\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})\big|\leq 2\Gamma A((1-\bm{s}_{i1})^{2}+Q_{i}),

(75)

where
\mathcal{T}_{i}
is the set of support indices.

Plugging in
\bm{h},\bm{\bar{h}}
in the bound above and assuming
\pi\leq 1
(w.l.o.g.), (
D
) is implied by the following stronger inequality

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell^{\prime}_{i}\cdot\left(6\Gamma A((1-\bm{s}_{i1})^{2}+Q_{i})+\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}(\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it})\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1+\pi}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell^{\prime}_{i}\cdot\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}(\bm{\bar{h}}_{i1}-\bm{\bar{h}}_{it})\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1+\pi}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell^{\prime}_{i}\cdot\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it}).

First, we claim that
0.5\pi\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})\geq 6\Gamma A((1-\bm{s}_{i1})^{2}+Q_{i})
for all
i\in[n]
. The proof of this claim directly follows the earlier argument, namely, following (
69
), (
70
), and (
71
) which leads to the choice

R\geq\frac{\max(2,\delta^{-1})}{\Theta}\log\left(\frac{C_{0}\cdot T\Gamma A}{\pi\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}}\right),

(76)

for some constant
C_{0}>0
. Using (
71
), we choose
C_{0}\geq 64\pi
to guarantee
R=R_{\pi}\geq\bar{R}_{\mu}
.

Following this control over the perturbation term
6\Gamma A((1-\bm{s}_{i1})^{2}+Q_{i})
, to conclude with the result, what remains is proving the comparison

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell^{\prime}_{i}\cdot\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}(\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it})\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})\leq-\frac{1+0.5\pi}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell^{\prime}_{i}\cdot\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it}).

(77)

To proceed, we split the problem into two scenarios.

Scenario 1:

\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}-\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}\leq\epsilon=\frac{\pi}{4A\Theta}
for some
\epsilon>0
. In this scenario, for any
t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}
and
i\in[n]
, we have

|\bm{h}_{it}-\bm{\bar{h}}_{it}|=|\bm{x}_{it}^{\top}({\bm{\bar{W}}}-\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}){\bm{z}}_{it}|\leq A\Theta\epsilon=\frac{\pi}{4}.

Consequently, we obtain

\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it}\leq\bm{\bar{h}}_{i1}-\bm{\bar{h}}_{it}+2A\Theta\epsilon=1+0.5\pi.

Similarly,
\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it}\geq 1-0.5\pi\geq 0.5
. Since all terms
\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it},\bm{s}_{it},\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it}
in (
77
) are nonnegative and
(\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it})\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})\leq(1+0.5\pi)\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})
, above implies the desired result in (
77
).

Scenario 2:

\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}-\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}\geq\epsilon=\frac{\pi}{4A\Theta}
. Since
{\bm{\bar{W}}}
is not (locally) max-margin, in this scenario, for some
i\in[n]
,
\nu=\nu(\epsilon)>0
, and
\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{i}
, we have that

\displaystyle\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{i\tau}\leq 1-2\nu.

Here
\tau=\arg\max_{\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{x}_{i\tau}{\bm{\bar{W}}}{\bm{z}}_{i}
denotes the nearest point to
\bm{h}_{i1}
(along the
{\bm{\bar{W}}}
direction). Note that a non-neighbor
t\in\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{i}
cannot be nearest because
{\bm{\bar{W}}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mu}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
and (
64
) holds. Recall that
\bm{s}_{i}=\mathbb{S}(\bar{R}\bm{h}_{i})
where
\bar{R}=\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}\Theta\geq R\Theta
. To proceed, let
\underline{\bm{h}}_{i}:=\min_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}:=\left\{i\in[n]:\underline{\bm{h}}_{i}\leq 1-2\nu\right\},\qquad[n]-\mathcal{I}:=\left\{i\in[n]:1-2\nu<\underline{\bm{h}}_{i}\right\}.

For all
i\in[n]-\mathcal{I}
,

\begin{split}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}(\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it})\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})&-(1+0.5\pi)\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})\\
&\leq\left(2A-(1+0.5\pi)\right)\Gamma\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i},~\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it}\geq 1+\frac{\pi}{2}}\bm{s}_{it}\\
&\leq\left(2A-(1+0.5\pi)\right)\Gamma Te^{-\bar{R}(1+\frac{\pi}{2})}\\
&\leq 2A\Gamma Te^{-\bar{R}(1+\frac{\pi}{2})}.\end{split}

(78)

For all
i\in\mathcal{I}
, split the tokens into two groups: Let
{\cal{N}}_{i}
be the group of tokens obeying
\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it}\leq 1-\nu
and
\mathcal{T}_{i}-{\cal{N}}_{i}
be the rest of the neighbors. Observe that

\frac{\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}-{\cal{N}}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}}{\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}}\leq T\frac{e^{\nu\bar{R}}}{e^{2\nu\bar{R}}}=Te^{-\bar{R}\nu}.

Using
|\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it}|\leq 2A=2\max_{i\in[n],t\in[T]}\|{\bm{k}_{it}}\|/\Theta
and
\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}=\min_{i\in[n]}\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{i}=\min_{i\in[n]}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\max_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{\gamma}_{it})
, observe that

\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}-{\cal{N}}_{i}}(\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it})\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})\leq\frac{2\Gamma ATe^{-\bar{R}\nu}}{\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it}).

Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}(\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it})\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t\in{\cal{N}}_{i}}(\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it})\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})+\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}-{\cal{N}}_{i}}(\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it})\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t\in{\cal{N}}_{i}}(1-\nu)\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})+\frac{2\Gamma ATe^{-\bar{R}\nu}}{\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\nu+\frac{2\Gamma ATe^{-\bar{R}\nu}}{\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}}\right)\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{2\Gamma ATe^{-\bar{R}\nu}}{\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}}\right)\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it}).

Hence, choosing

\displaystyle R\geq\frac{1}{\nu\Theta}\log\left(\frac{8\Gamma AT}{\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}\pi}\right)

(79)

results in that

\begin{split}&\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}(\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it})\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})-(1+\frac{\pi}{2})\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})\\
&\leq\left(\frac{2\Gamma ATe^{-\bar{R}\nu}}{\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}}-\frac{\pi}{2}\right)\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})\\
&\leq-\frac{\pi}{4}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})\\
&\leq-\frac{\pi}{4T}\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}e^{-\bar{R}(1-2\nu)}.\end{split}

(80)

Here, the last inequality follows from the fact that
\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}\bm{s}_{it}\geq\max_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}s_{it}\geq\frac{e^{-\bar{R}(1-2\nu)}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}e^{-\bar{R}(\bm{h}_{i1}-\bm{h}_{it})}}\geq e^{-\bar{R}(1-2\nu)}/T
.

From Assumption
A
, we have
c_{\min}\leq-\ell^{\prime}\leq c_{\max}
for some positive constants
c_{\min}
and
c_{\max}
. It follows from (
78
) and (
80
) that

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}^{n}\ell_{i}^{\prime}\cdot

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}(\bm{h}_{i1}-_{it})\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})-\sum_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{i}}(1+0.5\pi)\bm{s}_{it}(\bm{\gamma}_{i1}-\bm{\gamma}_{it})\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{\max}2A\Gamma T\Gamma e^{-\bar{R}(1+\frac{\pi}{2})}-\frac{c_{\min}}{nT}\cdot\frac{\pi\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}}{4}e^{-\bar{R}(1-2\nu)}

\displaystyle\leq 0.

Combing with (
79
), this is guaranteed by choosing

R\geq\max\left\{\frac{1}{\nu\Theta}\log\left(\frac{8\Gamma AT}{\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}\pi}\right),\frac{1}{(2\nu+\pi/2)\Theta}\log\left(\frac{8n\Gamma AT^{2}c_{\max}}{c_{\min}\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}\pi}\right)\right\},

where
\nu=\nu(\frac{\pi}{4A\Theta})
depends only on
\pi
and global problem variables.

Combining this with the prior
R
choice (
76
) (by taking maximum), we conclude with the statement.

D.1
Proof of Theorem
5

The proof of this theorem follows the proof of
[
TLZO23
, Theorem 3]
. Let us denote the initialization lower bound as
R^{0}_{\mu}:=R
, where
R
is given in the Theorem
5
’s statement. Consider an arbitrary value of
\epsilon\in(0,\mu/2)
and let
1/(1+\pi)=1-\epsilon
. We additionally denote
R_{\epsilon}\leftarrow R_{\pi}\vee 1/2
where
R_{\pi}
was defined in Lemma
14
. At initialization
\bm{W}(0)
, we set
\epsilon=\mu/2
to obtain
R^{0}_{\mu}=R_{\mu/2}
, and provide the proof in four steps:

Step 1: There are no stationary points within
\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R^{0}_{\mu}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
.
We begin by proving that there are no stationary points within
\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R^{0}_{\mu}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
. Let
(\mathcal{T}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
denote the sets of
support indices
as defined in Definition
2
. We define
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{i}=[T]-\mathcal{T}_{i}-\{\alpha_{i}\}
as the tokens that are non-
support indices
. Additionally, let
\mu
be defined as in (
63
). Then, since
R^{0}_{\mu}\geq\bar{R}_{\mu}
per Lemma
14
, we can apply Lemma
13
to find that: For all
\bm{V},\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mu}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
with
\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}\neq 0
and
\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}\geq R^{0}_{\mu}
, we have that
-\left<\bm{V},\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})\right>
is strictly positive.

Step 2:
It follows from Lemma
14
that, there exists
R_{\epsilon}\geq\bar{R}_{\mu}\vee 1/2
such that all
\bm{W}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R_{\epsilon}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
satisfy

\displaystyle\left\langle-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}),\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right\rangle\geq(1-\epsilon)\left\langle-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}),\frac{\bm{W}}{\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}}\right\rangle.

(81)

The argument below applies to a general
\epsilon\in(0,\mu/2)
. However, at initialization
\bm{W}(0)
, we set
\epsilon=\mu/2
and, recalling above, initialization lower bound was defined as
R^{0}_{\mu}:=R_{\mu/2}
. To proceed, for any
\epsilon\in(0,\mu/2)
, we will show that after gradient descent enters the conic set
\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R_{\epsilon}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
for the first time, it will never leave the set. Let
t_{\epsilon}
be the first time gradient descent enters
\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R_{\epsilon}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
. In
Step 4
, we will prove that such
t_{\epsilon}
is guaranteed to exist. Additionally, for
\epsilon\leftarrow\mu/2
, note that
t_{\epsilon}=0
i.e. the point of initialization.

Step 3: Updates remain inside the cone
\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R_{\epsilon}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
.

By leveraging the results from
Step 1
and
Step 2
, we demonstrate that the gradient iterates, with an appropriate constant step size, starting from
\bm{W}(k_{\epsilon})\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R_{\epsilon}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
, remain within this cone.

We proceed by induction. Suppose that the claim holds up to iteration
k\geq k_{\epsilon}
. This implies that
\bm{W}(k)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R_{\epsilon}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
. Hence, recalling cone definition, there exists scalar
\mu=\mu({\bm{\alpha}})\in(0,1)
and
R
such that
\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}\geq R
, and

\begin{split}\left\langle\frac{\bm{W}(k)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}},\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right\rangle\geq 1-\mu.\end{split}

For all
k\geq 1
, let

\displaystyle\rho(k):=-\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k)),\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right\rangle.

(82)

Note that
\rho(k)>0
due to
Step 1
. This together with the gradient descent update rule gives

\begin{split}\left\langle\frac{\bm{W}(k+1)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}},\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right\rangle&=\left\langle\frac{\bm{W}(k)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}-\frac{\eta}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k)),\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right\rangle\\
&\geq 1-\mu-\frac{\eta}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k)),\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right\rangle\\
&\geq 1-\mu+\frac{\eta\rho(k)(1-\epsilon)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}.\end{split}

(83a)

Note that from Lemma
13
, we have
\left\langle\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}(k)),\bm{W}(k)\right\rangle<0
which implies that
\|{\bm{W}(k+1)}\|_{F}\geq\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}
. This together with
R_{\epsilon}
definition and
\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}\geq 1/2
implies that

\displaystyle\|{\bm{W}(k+1)}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{{2\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}}\left(\|{\bm{W}(k+1)}\|_{F}^{2}+\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\left(2\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}^{2}-2\eta\left\langle\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}(k)),\bm{W}(k)\right\rangle+\eta^{2}\|{\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}(k))}\|_{F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}-\frac{\eta}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\left\langle\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}(k)),\bm{W}(k)\right\rangle+\eta^{2}\|{\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}(k))}\|_{F}^{2},

which gives

\begin{split}\frac{\|{\bm{W}(k+1)}\|_{F}}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}&\leq 1-\frac{\eta}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\left\langle\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}(k)),\frac{\bm{W}(k)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\right\rangle+\eta^{2}\frac{\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k))\|^{2}}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\\
&\leq 1-\frac{\eta}{(1-\epsilon)\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k)),\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right\rangle+\eta^{2}\frac{\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k))\|^{2}}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\\
&\leq 1+\frac{\eta\rho(k)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}+\frac{\eta^{2}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k))\|^{2}}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}=:C_{1}(\rho(k),\eta).\end{split}

(83b)

Here, the second inequality follows from (
81
) and (
82
).

Now, it follows from (
83a
) and (
83b
) that

\begin{split}\left\langle\frac{\bm{W}(k+1)}{\|\bm{W}(k+1)\|},\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}\|}\right\rangle&\geq\frac{1}{C_{1}(\rho(k),\eta)}\left(1-\mu+\frac{\eta\rho(k)(1-\epsilon)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\right)\\
&=1-\mu+\frac{1}{C_{1}(\rho(k),\eta)}\left((1-\mu)(1-C_{1}(\rho(k),\eta))+\frac{\eta\rho(k)(1-\epsilon)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\right)\\
&=1-\mu+\frac{\eta}{C_{1}(\rho(k),\eta)}\left((\mu-1)(\frac{\rho(k)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}+\frac{\eta\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k))\|^{2}}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}})+\frac{\rho(k)(1-\epsilon)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\right)\\
&=1-\mu+\frac{\eta}{C_{1}(\rho(k),\eta)}\left(\frac{\rho(k)(\mu-\epsilon)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}-\eta(1-\mu)\frac{\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k))\|^{2}}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\right)\\
&\geq 1-\mu,\end{split}

(84)

where the last inequality uses our choice of stepsize
\eta\leq 1/L_{W}
in Theorem
5
’s statement. Specifically, we need
\eta
to be small to ensure the last inequality. We will guarantee this by choosing a proper
R_{\epsilon}
in Lemma
14
. Specifically, Lemma
14
leaves the choice of
C_{0}
in
R_{\epsilon}
lower bound of (
76
) open (it can always be chosen larger). Here, by choosing
C_{0}\gtrsim 1/L_{\bm{W}}
will ensure
\eta\leq 1/L_{W}
works well.

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu}{2(1-\mu)(1-\frac{\mu}{2})}\frac{c}{C}\frac{\Theta}{\bar{A}}\frac{1}{\bar{A}CT}e^{R_{\mu}^{0}\Theta/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu-\epsilon}{1-\mu}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}\cdot\frac{c}{C}\cdot\frac{\Theta}{\bar{A}}\cdot\frac{1}{\bar{A}CT}e^{R_{\mu}^{0}\Theta/2}\leq\frac{(\mu-\epsilon)}{1-\mu}\frac{\rho(k)}{\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k))\|^{2}_{F}}.

(85)

Here, the first inequality uses our choice of
\epsilon\in(0,\mu/2)
(see
Step 2
), and the last inequality is obtained from Lemma
13
since

\displaystyle\frac{\rho(k)}{\|{\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k))}\|_{F}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}\left\langle\frac{\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k))}{\|{\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k))}\|_{F}},\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right\rangle\geq\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}\cdot\frac{c}{C}\cdot\frac{\Theta}{\bar{A}},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\|{\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k))}\|_{F}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\bar{A}C\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-\bm{s}_{i\alpha_{i}}\right)}\geq\frac{1}{\bar{A}CTe^{-R_{\mu}^{0}\Theta/2}}

for some data dependent constrants
c
and
C
,
\bar{A}=\max_{i\in[n],t,\tau\in[T]}\|{(\bm{x}_{it}-\bm{x}_{i\tau})}\|~\|{{\bm{z}}_{i}}\|
, and
\Theta=1/\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}
.

Next, we will demonstrate that the choice of
\eta
in (
D.1
) does indeed meet our step size condition as stated in the theorem, i.e.,
\eta\leq 1/L_{\bm{W}}
. Recall that
1/(1+\pi)=1-\epsilon
, which implies that
\pi=\epsilon/(1-\epsilon)
. Combining this with (
76
), we obtain:

\displaystyle R_{\pi}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\max(2,\delta^{-1})}{\Theta}\log\left(\frac{C_{0}T\Gamma A}{\pi\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}}\right),\quad\textnormal{where}\quad C_{0}\geq 64\pi.

(86)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow R_{\epsilon}\geq\frac{\max(2,\delta^{-1})}{\Theta}\log\left(\frac{(1-\epsilon)C_{0}T\Gamma A}{\epsilon\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}}\right),\quad\textnormal{where}\quad C_{0}\geq 64\frac{\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}.

(87)

On the other hand, at the initialization, we have
\epsilon=\mu/2
which implies that

\displaystyle R_{\mu}^{0}\geq\frac{\max(2,\delta^{-1})}{\Theta}\log\left(\frac{(2-\mu)C_{0}T\Gamma A}{\mu\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}}\right),\quad\textnormal{where}\quad C_{0}\geq 64\frac{\mu}{2(1-\frac{\mu}{2})}.

(88)

In the following, we will determine a lower bound on
C_{0}
such that our step size condition in Theorem
5
’s statement, i.e.,
\eta\leq 1/L_{\bm{W}}
, is satisfied. Note that for the choice of
\eta
in (
D.1
) to meet the condition
\eta\leq 1/L_{\bm{W}}
, the following condition must hold:

\frac{1}{L_{\bm{W}}}\leq\frac{\mu}{(2-\mu)}\frac{1}{C_{2}T}e^{R_{\mu}^{0}\Theta/2}\Rightarrow R_{\mu}^{0}\geq\frac{2}{\Theta}\log\left(\frac{1}{L_{\bm{W}}}\frac{2-\mu}{\mu}C_{2}T\right).

(89)

where

C_{2}=(1-\mu)\frac{\bar{A}^{2}C^{2}}{\Theta c}
.

This together with (
88
) implies that

\displaystyle\frac{C_{0}\Gamma A}{\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}}

\displaystyle\geq(1-\mu)\frac{C_{2}}{L_{\bm{W}}}\Rightarrow C_{0}\geq\max\left(\frac{(1-\mu)C_{2}}{L_{\bm{W}}}\frac{\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{\min}}{\Gamma A},\frac{64\mu}{2-\mu}\right).

(90)

Therefore, with this lower bound on
C_{0}
, the step size bound in (
D.1
) is sufficiently large to ensure that
\eta\leq 1/L_{\bm{W}}
guarantees (
84
).

Hence, it follows from (
84
) that
\bm{W}(k+1)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R_{\epsilon}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
.

Step 4: The correlation of
\bm{W}(k)
and
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}
increases over
k
.

From Step 3, we have that all iterates remain within the initial conic set i.e.
\bm{W}(k)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R^{0}_{\mu}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
for all
k\geq 0
. Note that it follows from Lemma
13
that
\left<\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}),\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}/\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}\right><0
, for any finite
\bm{W}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R^{0}_{\mu}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
. Hence, there are no finite critical points
\bm{W}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R^{0}_{\mu}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
, for which
\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{W})=0
. Now, based on Lemma
9
, which guarantees that
\nabla{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}(k))\rightarrow 0
, this
implies that
\left\|\bm{W}\left(t\right)\right\|_{F}\rightarrow\infty
. Consequently, for any choice of
\epsilon\in(0,\mu/2)
there is an iteration
k_{\epsilon}
such that, for all
k\geq k_{\epsilon}
,
\bm{W}(k)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R_{\epsilon}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
. Once within
\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R_{\epsilon}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}})
, multiplying both sides (
81
) by the stepsize
\eta
and using the gradient descent update, we get

\begin{split}\left\langle\bm{W}(k+1)-\bm{W}(k),\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right\rangle&\geq(1-\epsilon)\left\langle\bm{W}(k+1)-\bm{W}(k),\frac{\bm{W}(k)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\right\rangle\\
&=\frac{(1-\epsilon)}{2\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\left(\|{\bm{W}(k+1)}\|_{F}^{2}-\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}^{2}-\|{\bm{W}(k+1)-\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}^{2}\right)\\
&\geq(1-\epsilon)\left(\frac{1}{2\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}}\left(\|{\bm{W}(k+1)}\|_{F}^{2}-\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}^{2}\right)-\|{\bm{W}(k+1)-\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}^{2}\right)\\
&\geq(1-\epsilon)\left(\|{\bm{W}(k+1)}\|_{F}-\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}-\|{\bm{W}(k+1)-\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}^{2}\right)\\
&\geq(1-\epsilon)\Big(\|{\bm{W}(k+1)}\|_{F}-\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}-2\eta\left(\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k))-\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k+1))\right)\Big).\end{split}

Here, the second inequality is obtained from
\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}\geq 1/2
; the third inequality follows since for any
a,b>0
, we have
(a^{2}-b^{2})/(2b)-(a-b)\geq 0
; and the last inequality uses Lemma
9
.

Summing the above inequality over
k\geq k_{\epsilon}
gives

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\bm{W}(k)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}},\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right\rangle\geq 1-\epsilon+\frac{C(\epsilon,\eta)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}},\qquad\bm{W}(k)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R_{\epsilon}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}),

where
\mathcal{L}_{\star}\leq\mathcal{L}\left(\bm{W}\left(k\right)\right)
for all
k\geq 0
, and

C(\epsilon,\eta)=\left\langle\bm{W}(k_{\epsilon}),\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right\rangle-(1-\epsilon)\|{\bm{W}(k_{\epsilon})}\|_{F}-2\eta(1-\epsilon)(\mathcal{L}(\bm{W}(k_{\epsilon}))-\mathcal{L}_{\star}).

Consequently, as
k\rightarrow\infty

\displaystyle\liminf_{k\to\infty}\left\langle\frac{\bm{W}(k)}{\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}},\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}}\right\rangle\geq 1-\epsilon,\qquad\bm{W}(k)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mu,R_{\epsilon}}(\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}).

Since
\epsilon\in(0,\mu/2)
is arbitrary, we get
\bm{W}(k)/\|{\bm{W}(k)}\|_{F}\to\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}/\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}
.

\hfill\blacksquare

Appendix E
Convergence of Regularization Path for Sequence-to-Sequence Setting

In this section, we provide proofs for the regularization path analysis. We first provide a more general formulation of the optimization problem that allows for regressing multiple token outputs. To distinguish from (
W-ERM
)&(
KQ-ERM
), let us call this more general version Sequence Empirical Risk Minimization (SERM).

Problem definition:

Rather than a single input sequence
{\bm{X}}
, let us allow for two input sequences
{\bm{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times d}
and
\bm{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times d}
with
(\bm{x}_{t})_{t=1}^{T}
and
({\bm{z}}_{k})_{k=1}^{K}
. The cross-attention admits
{\bm{X}},\bm{Z}
and outputs
K
tokens. We will also allow for
K
separate prediction heads
(h_{k})_{k=1}^{K}
for individual cross attention outputs which strictly generalizes the setting where we used single prediction head
h(\cdot)
. Denote the training labels associated to each token as
{\bm{Y}}=(Y_{ik})_{i=1}^{K}
. Given
n
samples
({\bm{Y}}_{i},{\bm{X}}_{i},\bm{Z}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
, for a decreasing loss function
\ell(\cdot)
, minimize the empirical risk by the prediction of first attention output either univariate (
\bm{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
) or bivariate (
{\bm{K}},{\bm{Q}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
) fashion:

\displaystyle{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\ell(Y_{ik}\cdot h_{k}({\bm{X}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbb{S}({\bm{X}}_{i}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}_{ik}))),

(SERM-W)

\displaystyle{\cal{L}}({\bm{K}},{\bm{Q}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(Y_{ik}\cdot h_{k}({\bm{X}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbb{S}({\bm{X}}_{i}{\bm{K}}{\bm{Q}}^{\top}{\bm{z}}_{ik}))).

(SERM-KQ)

In order to recover the single-output self-attention model, we can simply set
K=1
and
{\bm{z}}_{i1}\leftarrow\bm{x}_{i1}
and
h_{k}\leftarrow h
. To proceed, we introduce the more general version of the (
Att-SVM
) problem, which we refer to as
Sequential Cross-Attention SVM
to preserve consistent phrasing. Suppose
{\bm{K}},{\bm{Q}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
with
m\leq d
and let
\mathcal{R}_{m}
denote the set of rank-
m
matrices in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
. Given indices
{\bm{\alpha}}=(\alpha_{ik})_{{ik}=(1,1)}^{(n,K)}
, consider the SVM with
{\diamond}
-norm constraint

\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\diamond},{\bm{\alpha}}}\in\arg\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{R}_{m}}\|\bm{W}\|_{\diamond}\quad\text{s.t.}\quad\min_{t\neq\alpha_{ik}}(\bm{x}_{i\alpha_{ik}}-\bm{x}_{it})^{\top}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}_{ik}\geq 1\quad\forall\quad i\in[n],k\in[K].

(SAtt-SVM)

When solution is non-unique, we denote the solution set by
{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}({\bm{\alpha}})
. In what follows, we denote
{\bm{F}}_{ikt}:=\bm{x}_{it}{\bm{z}}_{{ik}}^{\top}
and given
{\bm{\alpha}}
, we denote
\bm{x}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}=\bm{x}_{i\alpha_{ik}}
and
{\bm{F}}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}:=\bm{x}_{i\alpha_{ik}}{\bm{z}}_{{ik}}^{\top}
. With this notation, we can equivalently write

\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\diamond},{\bm{\alpha}}}\in\arg\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{R}_{m}}\|\bm{W}\|_{\diamond}\quad\text{s.t.}\quad\min_{t\neq\alpha_{ik}}\left<{\bm{F}}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}-{\bm{F}}_{ikt},\bm{W}\right>\geq 1\quad\forall~i\in[n],k\in[K].

(SAtt-SVM’)

Definition 4
(
Support indices
and Locally-Optimal Indices)

Fix token indices
{\bm{\alpha}}=(\alpha_{ik})_{ik=(1,1)}^{(n,k)}
for which (
SAtt-SVM
) is feasible to obtain
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}:=\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\diamond},{\bm{\alpha}}}
. Define token scores as

\bm{\gamma}_{ikt}=Y_{ik}\cdot h_{k}(\bm{x}_{it}),\quad\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}:=\bm{\gamma}_{ik\alpha_{ik}}=Y_{ik}\cdot h_{k}(\bm{x}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}).

Consider tokens
\mathcal{T}_{ik}\subset[T]
such that
\left<{\bm{F}}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}-{\bm{F}}_{ikt},\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}\right>=1
for all
t\in\mathcal{T}_{ik}
.
\mathcal{T}_{ik}
is allowed to be an empty set. We refer to
\mathcal{T}_{ik}
as
support indices
of
{\bm{F}}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}=\bm{x}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}{\bm{z}}_{ik}^{\top}
and define its complement
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{ik}=[T]-\mathcal{T}_{ik}-\{\alpha_{ik}\}
. Additionally, token indices
{\bm{\alpha}}=(\alpha_{ik})_{ik=(1,1)}^{(n,k)}
are called locally-optimal if for all
i\in[n],k\in[K]
and
t\in\mathcal{T}_{ik}
, token scores obey
\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}>\bm{\gamma}_{ikt}
. Associated
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}
is called a locally-optimal direction. Finally, let
\texttt{opt}_{ik}\in\arg\max_{t\in[T]}\bm{\gamma}_{ikt}
be the optimal indices and define the associated
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}(\texttt{opt})
to be a globally-optimal direction.

Lemma 15
(Mapping regularization path of
({\bm{K}},{\bm{Q}})
to
\bm{W}
)

Let
{\bm{K}},{\bm{Q}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
and consider regularization path solutions of (
SERM-W
) and (
SERM-KQ
)

\displaystyle{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{R}\in\underset{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{R}_{m}:\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\star}\leq R}{\arg\min}{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})

(91)

\displaystyle{\bm{\bar{K}}}_{R},{\bm{\bar{Q}}}_{R}\in\underset{\|{{\bm{K}}}\|_{F}^{2}+\|{{\bm{Q}}}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 2R}{\arg\min}{\cal{L}}({\bm{K}},{\bm{Q}}).

(92)

For all
R\geq 0
, there is a one-to-one map between the set of solutions
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{R}
of (
91
) and
{\bm{\bar{K}}}_{R}{\bm{\bar{Q}}}_{R}^{\top}
of (
92
).

Proof.
To prove the mapping, first fix a
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{R}
solution with rank
m
, set
{\cal{L}}_{F}={\cal{L}}({\bm{\bar{W}}}_{R})
and show the existence of
{\bm{K}},{\bm{Q}}
with
{\bm{K}}{\bm{Q}}^{\top}={\bm{\bar{W}}}_{R}
feasible for (
92
) and
{\cal{L}}({\bm{K}},{\bm{Q}})\leq{\cal{L}}_{F}
. Use the singular value decomposition
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{R}={\bm{U}}{\bm{{\Sigma}}}{\bm{V}}^{\top}
with
{\bm{{\Sigma}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
being diagonal matrix of singular values. Set
{\bm{K}}={\bm{U}}\sqrt{{\bm{{\Sigma}}}}
and
{\bm{Q}}={\bm{V}}\sqrt{{\bm{{\Sigma}}}}
. Observe that
{\bm{K}}{\bm{Q}}^{\top}=\bm{W}
and

\|{{\bm{K}}}\|_{F}^{2}=\|{{\bm{Q}}}\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sqrt{{\bm{{\Sigma}}}_{ii}}^{2}=\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{R}}\|_{\star}\leq R.

Thus,
{\bm{K}},{\bm{Q}}
achieves
{\cal{L}}({\bm{K}},{\bm{Q}})={\cal{L}}_{F}
. Conversely, given
{\bm{\bar{K}}}_{R},{\bm{\bar{Q}}}_{R}
with
{\cal{L}}_{\star}={\cal{L}}({\bm{\bar{K}}}_{R},{\bm{\bar{Q}}}_{R})
,
\bm{W}={\bm{\bar{K}}}_{R}{\bm{\bar{Q}}}_{R}^{\top}
obeys
{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})={\cal{L}}_{\star}
and, using the standard nuclear norm inequality, we have

\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\star}=\|{{\bm{\bar{K}}}_{R}{\bm{\bar{Q}}}_{R}^{\top}}\|_{\star}\leq\frac{1}{2}(\|{{\bm{\bar{K}}}_{R}}\|_{F}^{2}+\|{{\bm{\bar{Q}}}_{R}}\|_{F}^{2})=R.

This shows
\bm{W}
is feasible for (
91
). Combining the two findings above, we find that optimal costs are equal (
{\cal{L}}_{\star}={\cal{L}}_{F}
) and for any
({\bm{\bar{K}}}_{R},{\bm{\bar{Q}}}_{R})
solution there exists a
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{R}
solution and vice versa.

To proceed with our analysis, let us define the set of optimal solutions
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\bm{\alpha}}}:=\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\diamond},{\bm{\alpha}}}
to (
SAtt-SVM
). Let us denote this set by
{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\bm{\alpha}}}:={\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\diamond}({\bm{\alpha}})
. Note that, if the
{\diamond}
-norm is not strongly-convex,
{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\bm{\alpha}}}
may not be singleton.

E.1
Local regularization path and proof of Theorem
7

We first recall
local regularization path
which solves the
{\diamond}
-norm-constrained problem over a conic slice
\mathcal{C}
, namely
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)=\min_{\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}\leq R,\bm{W}\in\mathcal{C}}{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})
. We will show that proper local RP directionally converge to locally-optimal directions. Setting the cone to be the rank-
m
manifold
\mathcal{R}_{m}
(and more specifically the set of all matrices
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
), this will also establish the convergence of global RP to the globally-optimal direction.

Our cone definition
\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})
is induced by a token selection
{\bm{\alpha}}=(\alpha_{ik})_{ik=(1,1)}^{(n,k)}
and has a simple interpretation: It prioritizes tokens with lower score than
{\bm{\alpha}}
over tokens with high-score than
{\bm{\alpha}}
. This way lower score tokens create a barrier for
{\bm{\alpha}}
and prevents optimization to move towards higher score tokens.

Definition 5
(Low&High Score Tokens and Separating Cone)

Given
\alpha\in[T]
, input sequence
{\bm{X}}
with label
Y
,
h(\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, and score
\bm{\gamma}_{t}=Y\cdot h(\bm{x}_{t})
for all
t\in[T]
, define the low and high score tokens as

\texttt{low}^{\alpha}({\bm{X}})=\left\{t\in[T]{~\big|~}\bm{\gamma}_{t}<\bm{\gamma}_{\alpha}\},\quad\texttt{high}^{\alpha}({\bm{X}})=\{t\in[T]-\{\alpha\}{~\big|~}\bm{\gamma}_{t}\geq\bm{\gamma}_{\alpha}\right\}.

For input
{\bm{X}}_{ik}
and index
\alpha_{ik}
, we use the shorthand notations
\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha},\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}
. Finally define
\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})
as

\displaystyle\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})=\left\{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{R}_{m}{~\big|~}\min_{i\in[n]}\max_{t\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}}\min_{\tau\in\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}}\left<{\bm{F}}_{ikt}-{\bm{F}}_{ik\tau},\bm{W}\right>\geq\epsilon\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}\right\}.

(93)

Lemma 16

Consider the cone definition of (
93
) and suppose an SVM solution
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}
exists. If indices
{\bm{\alpha}}
are locally-optimal,
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}\in\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})
for all sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
. Otherwise,
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}\not\in\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})
for all
\epsilon>0
. Additionally, suppose optimal indices
\texttt{opt}_{ik}\in\arg\max_{t\in[T]}\bm{\gamma}_{ikt}
are unique. Then,
\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}(\texttt{opt})=\mathcal{R}_{m}
.

Proof.
Suppose
{\bm{\alpha}}
is locally optimal. Observe that, thanks to local optimality,
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}
obeys

\min_{t\in\mathcal{T}_{ik}}\left<{\bm{F}}_{ikt},\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}\right>>\max_{\tau\not\in\mathcal{T}_{ik}\cup\{\alpha_{ik}\}}\left<{\bm{F}}_{ik\tau},\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}\right>,

for all
i\in[n]
. Next, observe that
\mathcal{T}_{ik}\subseteq\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}
and
\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}\subseteq\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{ik}=[T]-\mathcal{T}_{ik}-\{\alpha_{ik}\}
. Thus, the inequality (
93
) holds for small enough
\epsilon>0
.

Conversely, suppose
{\bm{\alpha}}
is not locally-optimal. Fix
support index

t\in\mathcal{T}_{ik}
with
t\in\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}
. Since
t\in\mathcal{T}_{ik}
, observe that

\left<{\bm{F}}_{ikt},\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}\right>\geq\max_{\tau\neq\alpha_{ik}}\left<{\bm{F}}_{ik\tau},\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}\right>.

In other words, for this
i\in[n]
, we found

\max_{\tau\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}}\left<{\bm{F}}_{ik\tau}-{\bm{F}}_{ikt},\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}\right>\leq 0,

violating (
93
) definition for any
\epsilon>0
. To show the final claim, observe that, setting
{\bm{\alpha}}:=\texttt{opt}
, we have that
\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}=\emptyset
for all
i\in[n]
as
\texttt{opt}_{ik}
are unique optimal indices. Thus, there is no constraint enforced on the cone definition in (
93
) making it equal to the rank-
m
manifold
\mathcal{R}_{m}
.

Our main assumption regarding prediction head is a monotonicity condition which is a strict generalization of linearity: We ask for
h
to preserve the order of token scores under convex combinations.

Assumption E
(
h
preserves the top score)

The functions
(h_{k})_{k=1}^{K}
are
L_{h}
-Lipschitz in Euclidean distance. Given
{\bm{\alpha}}=(\alpha_{ik})_{ik=(1,1)}^{(n,k)}
, there exists a scalar
c:=c_{\bm{\alpha}}>0
such that for all
i\in[n],k\in[K]
the following holds: Consider any convex combination

\bm{x}(\bm{s})=\sum_{t\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}\cup\{\alpha_{ik}\}}s_{t}\cdot\bm{x}_{it}\quad\text{where}\quad\sum_{t\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}\cup\{\alpha_{ik}\}}s_{t}=1,~s_{t}\geq 0.

We have that
Y_{ik}\cdot h_{k}(\bm{x}(\bm{s}))\leq\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}-c(1-s_{\alpha_{ik}})
where
\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}=Y_{ik}\cdot h_{k}(\bm{x}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{{ik}})
is the score of
\alpha_{ik}
.

This condition states that
convex combinations of tokens with scores lower than
\alpha_{ik}
cannot achieve a score higher than
\alpha_{ik}
. Here,
1-s_{\alpha_{ik}}
term denotes the total share of non-optimal tokens. We require this condition to hold over the training dataset rather than the full domain
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Crucially, it is a strict generalization of the linearity assumption: Any linear
h_{k}
satisfies Assumption
E
by setting
c_{\bm{\alpha}}>0
to be the difference between the score of
\alpha_{ik}
and the largest score within
\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}
i.e.

\displaystyle c_{\bm{\alpha}}:=\min_{i\in[n],k\in[K]}\{\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}-\max_{t\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}}\bm{\gamma}_{ikt}\}>0.

(94)

This can be seen by writing
h_{k}(\bm{x}(\bm{s}))=\sum_{t\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}\cup\{\alpha_{ik}\}}s_{t}\cdot\bm{\gamma}_{ikt}=\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}+\sum_{t\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}}s_{t}\cdot(\bm{\gamma}_{ikt}-\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik})\leq\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}-c_{\bm{\alpha}}(1-s_{\alpha_{ik}})
.
To provide a nonlinear example, consider the setting all labels are
Y_{ik}=1
and
h
is an arbitrary convex function. Thanks to convexity, we can write
h(\bm{x}(\bm{s}))\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}s_{t}\cdot h(\bm{x}_{t})=\sum_{t\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}\cup\{\alpha_{ik}\}}s_{t}\cdot\bm{\gamma}_{ikt}
. Thus, we can use the same choice of
c_{\bm{\alpha}}
in (
94
).

We remark that, in Section
6
, we derive formulae describing general inductive bias of attention without enforcing any assumption on
h
. These formulae allow for arbitrary output tokens generated by the transformer model trained by gradient descent. This includes the setting where gradient descent selects and composes multiple tokens from each sequence rather than a single token
\alpha_{ik}
.

The following result is our main theorem regarding the convergence of regularization path to locally-optimal directions when restricted over the cone
\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})
.

Theorem 9
(Convergence of Local Regularization Path)

Suppose (
SAtt-SVM
) is feasible and
{\bm{\alpha}}=(\alpha_{ik})_{ik=(1,1)}^{(n,k)}
are locally-optimal token indices. Suppose Assumptions
A
&
E
hold. Recall
\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})
of (
93
) and consider the norm-constrained cone

\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon,R_{0}}^{\diamond}:=\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})\bigcap\{\bm{W}{~\big|~}\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}\geq R_{0}\}.

Define the conic regularization path
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)=\min_{\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon,R_{0}}^{\diamond},\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}\leq R}{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})
. Let
{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\bm{\alpha}}}
be its set of minima and
\Xi_{\diamond}>0
be the associated margin i.e.
\Xi_{\diamond}=1/\|{{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\bm{\alpha}}}}\|_{\diamond}
. For any sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
and sufficiently large
R_{0}={\cal{O}}(1/\epsilon)>0
,
\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\texttt{dist}\left(\frac{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}{R\Xi_{\diamond}},{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right)=0
. Additionally, suppose optimal indices
\texttt{opt}=(\texttt{opt}_{ik})_{ik=(1,1)}^{(n,k)}
are unique and set
{\bm{\alpha}}\leftarrow\texttt{opt}
. Then, the same RP convergence guarantee holds with
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon,R_{0}}^{\diamond}=\mathcal{R}_{m}
.

Proof.
We will prove that
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
is the optimal direction and also
\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\rightarrow\infty
. Define the absolute constant

c_{\diamond}=\min_{\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}=1}\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}.

This guarantees that for any
\bm{W}
we have
\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}\geq c_{\diamond}\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}
. Also denote
\varepsilon_{\diamond}=c_{\diamond}\epsilon
. Let us first determine the
\epsilon
parameter: Fix
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}\in{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\bm{\alpha}}}
. For general
{\bm{\alpha}}
, we can choose any
\epsilon>0
that is sufficiently small to guarantee
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}\in\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})
based on Lemma
16
. For
{\bm{\alpha}}=\texttt{opt}
, our analysis will entirely avoid using
\epsilon
, specifically, observe that
\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})=\mathcal{R}_{m}
based on Lemma
16
.

Step 1:
Let us first prove that
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
achieves the optimal risk as
R\rightarrow\infty
– rather than problem having finite optima. Define norm-normalized
\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}=\Xi_{\diamond}\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}
. Note that
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}
separates tokens
{\bm{\alpha}}
from rest of the tokens for each
i,k\in[n]\times[K]
. Thus, we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}{\cal{L}}({\bm{\bar{W}}}(R))\leq\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}{\cal{L}}(R\cdot\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}):={\cal{L}}_{\star}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\ell(\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}).

(95)

On the other hand, for any choice of
\bm{W}\in\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})
, set
\bm{x}^{\bm{W}}_{ik}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{S}({\bm{X}}_{i}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}_{ik})_{t}\bm{x}_{t}
. Set softmax probabilities
\bm{s}^{({ik})}=\mathbb{S}({\bm{X}}_{i}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}_{ik})
. Recalling
\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha},\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}
definitions, we can decompose the attention features as

\displaystyle\bm{x}^{\bm{W}}_{ik}=\bm{s}^{({ik})}_{\alpha_{ik}}\bm{x}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}+\sum_{t\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}}\bm{s}^{({ik})}_{t}\bm{x}_{it}+\sum_{\tau\in\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}}\bm{s}^{({ik})}_{\tau}\bm{x}_{{i\tau}}.

(96)

When
{\bm{\alpha}}=\texttt{opt}
, note that we simply have
\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}=\emptyset
. This will be important for setting
R_{0}=0
and
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon,R_{0}}^{\diamond}=\mathcal{R}_{m}
in the proof for
opt
indices.

Set
\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{ikt}=\bm{\gamma}_{ikt}-\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}=Y_{ik}\cdot(h_{k}(\bm{x}_{it})-h_{k}(\bm{x}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}))
. Building on
L_{h}
-Lipschitzness of the prediction head
h_{k}(\cdot)
, we define

\displaystyle B:=\max_{i\in[n],k\in[K]}\max_{t,\tau\in[T]}L_{h}\cdot\|{\bm{x}_{it}-\bm{x}_{i\tau}}\|\geq|\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{ikt}|.

(97)

Define
P^{ik}:=\sum_{t\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}}\bm{s}^{({ik})}_{t}
,
Q^{ik}:=\sum_{t\in\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}}\bm{s}^{({ik})}_{t}
, and
\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{ik}=Y_{ik}\cdot h_{k}(\bm{x}^{\bm{W}}_{ik})
. Also set temporary variables
\bm{x}^{\prime}=(\bm{s}^{({ik})}_{\alpha_{ik}}+Q^{ik})\bm{x}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}+\sum_{t\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}}\bm{s}^{({ik})}_{t}\bm{x}_{it}
and
\bm{\gamma}^{\prime}=Y_{ik}\cdot h_{k}(\bm{x}^{\bm{W}}_{ik})
.
Using Assumption
E
on
\bm{x}^{\prime}
and noticing
P^{ik}=1-\bm{s}^{({ik})}_{\alpha_{ik}}-Q^{ik}
, observe that

|\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{ik}-\bm{\gamma}^{\prime}|\leq BQ^{ik}\quad\text{and}\quad\bm{\gamma}^{\prime}\leq\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}-c_{\bm{\alpha}}P^{ik}.

Recall from (
94
) that, when
h_{k}
are linear functions,
c_{\bm{\alpha}}
can be chosen as

\displaystyle c_{\bm{\alpha}}:=\min_{i\in[n],k\in[K]}\min_{t\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}}-\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{ikt}>0.

To summarize, applying Assumption
E
, we obtain the following score inequalities

\displaystyle\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{ik}\leq\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}-c_{\bm{\alpha}}P^{ik}+BQ^{ik},

(98)

\displaystyle|\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{ik}-\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}|\leq L_{h}\|{\bm{x}^{\bm{W}}_{ik}-\bm{x}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}}\|\leq L_{h}\sum_{t\neq\alpha_{ik}}\bm{s}^{({ik})}_{t}\|{\bm{x}_{ikt}-\bm{x}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}}\|\leq B(1-\bm{s}^{({ik})}_{\alpha_{ik}}).

(99)

We will use the
\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{ik}-\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}
term in (
98
) to evaluate
\bm{W}
against the reference loss (
95
). Let
{\bm{a}}^{({ik})}={\bm{X}}_{i}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}_{ik}
. Now since
\bm{W}\in\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})
, there exists
t\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}
obeying
{\bm{a}}^{({ik})}_{t}-\max_{\tau\in\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}}{\bm{a}}^{({ik})}_{\tau}\geq\epsilon\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}\geq\varepsilon_{\diamond}\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}
. Denote
D^{ik}:=(\sum_{t\in[T]}e^{{\bm{a}}^{({ik})}_{t}})^{-1}
to be the softmax denominator i.e. sum of exponentials. We find that,

\displaystyle Q^{ik}=\sum_{\tau\in\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}}\bm{s}^{({ik})}_{\tau}=D^{ik}\sum_{\tau\in\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}}e^{{\bm{a}}^{({ik})}_{\tau}}\leq D^{ik}Te^{{\bm{a}}^{({ik})}_{t}-\epsilon\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}}\leq Te^{-\varepsilon_{\diamond}\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}}P^{ik}.

(100)

Consequently, the score difference obeys

\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{ik}-\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}\leq BQ^{ik}-c_{\bm{\alpha}}P^{ik}\leq(BTe^{-\varepsilon_{\diamond}\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}}-c_{\bm{\alpha}})P^{ik}.

Above, the right hand side is strictly negative as soon as
\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}\geq R_{0}:=\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\diamond}}\log\frac{BT}{c_{\bm{\alpha}}}
. Note that, this condition applies to all
(i,k)\in[n]\times[K]
pairs uniformly for the same
R_{0}
. Consequently, for any
\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}\geq R_{0}
, for all
i,k
and
\bm{W}\in\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})
, we have that
\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{ik}<\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}
. Additionally, when
{\bm{\alpha}}=\texttt{opt}
, note that
Q^{ik}=0
since
\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}=\emptyset
. Thus,
R_{0}=0
suffices to ensure
\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{ik}<\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}
. Using the strictly-decreasing nature of
\ell
, we conclude with the fact that for all (finite)
\bm{W}\in\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})
,

{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\ell(\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{ik})>{\cal{L}}_{\star}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\ell(\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik}),

which implies
\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\rightarrow\infty
.

Step 2:
To proceed, we show that
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
converges in direction to
{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\bm{\alpha}}}
. Suppose this is not the case i.e. convergence fails. We will obtain a contradiction by showing that
\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R}=R\cdot\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}
achieves a strictly superior loss compared to
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
. Also define the normalized parameter
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)=\frac{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}{R\Xi_{\diamond}}
and
\bm{W}^{\prime}=\frac{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}{\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\Xi_{\diamond}}
. Note that
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)
is obtained by scaling down
\bm{W}^{\prime}
since
\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\leq R
and
\bm{W}^{\prime}
obeys
\|{\bm{W}^{\prime}}\|_{\diamond}=\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}}\|_{\diamond}
.

Since
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)
fails to converge to
{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\bm{\alpha}}}
, for some
\delta>0
, there exists arbitrarily large
R>0
such that
\texttt{dist}\left({\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R),{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right)\geq\delta
. This translates to the suboptimality in terms of margin constraints as follows: First, distance with respect to the
{\diamond}
-norm obeys
\texttt{dist}_{\diamond}\left({\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R),{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right)\geq\delta
for some updated
\delta\leftarrow c_{\diamond}\delta
. Secondly, using triangle inequality,

\text{This implies that either~~~}\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\leq\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}}\|_{\diamond}-\delta/2\text{~~~or~~~}\texttt{dist}_{\diamond}\left(\bm{W}^{\prime},{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right)\geq\delta/2.

In either scenario,
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)
strictly violates one of the margin constraints of (
SAtt-SVM
): If
\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\leq\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}}\|_{\diamond}-\delta/2
, then, since the optimal SVM objective is
\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}}\|_{\diamond}
, there exists a constraint
(i,k)
for which
\left<{\bm{F}}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{{ik}}-{\bm{F}}_{{ikt}},{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)\right>\leq 1-\frac{\delta}{2\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}}\|_{\diamond}}
. If
\texttt{dist}_{\diamond}\left(\bm{W}^{\prime},{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right)\geq\delta/2
, then,
\bm{W}^{\prime}
has same SVM objective but it is strictly bounded away from the solution set. Thus, for some
\epsilon:=\epsilon(\delta)>0
,
\bm{W}^{\prime}
and its scaled down version
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)
strictly violate an SVM constraint achieving margin
\leq 1-\epsilon
. Without losing generality, suppose
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)
violates the first constraint. Thus, for a properly updated
\delta>0
(that is function of the initial
\delta>0
) and for
(i,k)=(1,1)
and some
support index

\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{11}
,

\displaystyle\left<{\bm{F}}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{{11}}-{\bm{F}}_{{11}t},{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)\right>\leq 1-\delta.

(101)

Now, we will argue that this will lead to a contradiction by proving
{\cal{L}}(\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R})<{\cal{L}}({\bm{\bar{W}}}(R))
for sufficiently large
R
.

To obtain the result, we establish a refined softmax probability control as in Step 1 by studying distance to
{\cal{L}}_{\star}
. Following (
98
), denote the score function at
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
via
\bm{\gamma}^{R}_{ik}=\bm{\gamma}_{ik}^{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}
as shorthand notation. Similarly, let
\bm{s}^{R}_{ik}=\mathbb{S}({\bm{a}}^{R}_{ik})
with
{\bm{a}}^{R}_{ik}={\bm{X}}_{i}{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R){\bm{z}}_{ik}
. Set the corresponding notation for the reference parameter
\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R}
as
\bm{\gamma}^{\star}_{ik},\bm{s}^{\star}_{ik},{\bm{a}}^{\star}_{ik}
.

Critically, recall the above inequalities (
100
) that applies to both
\bm{W}\in\{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R),\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R}\}\subset\texttt{cone}_{\epsilon}({\bm{\alpha}})
for an index
(i,k)
and
support index

t\in\mathcal{T}_{ik}

\displaystyle Q^{ik}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau\in\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}}\bm{s}_{ik\tau}=D^{ik}\sum_{\tau\in\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}}e^{{\bm{a}}_{{ik\tau}}}

\displaystyle\leq D^{ik}Te^{{\bm{a}}_{ikt}-\varepsilon_{\diamond}\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}}\leq Te^{-\varepsilon_{\diamond}\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}}P^{ik}\leq Te^{-\varepsilon_{\diamond}\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}}(1-\bm{s}_{{ik}\alpha_{ik}}),

(102)

where
P^{ik}=\sum_{\tau\in\texttt{low}_{ik}^{\alpha}}\bm{s}_{{ik\tau}}
and
P^{ik}+Q^{ik}=1-\bm{s}_{{ik}\alpha_{ik}}
.

Note that, setting
R_{0}\geq{\cal{O}}(1/\varepsilon_{\diamond})={\cal{O}}(1/\epsilon)
, we guarantee that, for any
(i,k)\in[n]\times[K]

\displaystyle P^{ik}\geq Q^{ik}\implies P^{ik}\geq 0.5(1-\bm{s}_{{ik}\alpha_{ik}}).

(103)

Additionally, when
{\bm{\alpha}}=\texttt{opt}
, note that
Q^{ik}=0
since
\texttt{high}_{ik}^{\alpha}=\emptyset
. Thus,
R_{0}=0
suffices to ensure (
103
).

To proceed, recall that
R\geq\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\geq R_{0}
by definition since
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)\in\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon,R_{0}}^{\diamond}
and recall
\Xi_{\diamond}:=1/\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}}\|_{\diamond}
. Equipped with these, we note the following softmax inequalities on the selected tokens
\alpha_{ik}

\displaystyle\bm{s}^{\star}_{{ik}\alpha_{ik}}\geq\frac{1}{1+Te^{-R\Xi_{\diamond}}}\geq 1-Te^{-R\Xi_{\diamond}}\quad\text{for all}\quad(i,k)\in[n]\times[K],

(104)

\displaystyle s^{R}_{{ik}\alpha_{ik}}\leq\frac{1}{1+e^{-(1-\delta)\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\Xi_{\diamond}}}\leq\frac{1}{1+e^{-(1-\delta)R\Xi_{\diamond}}}\quad\text{for}\quad(i,k)=(1,1).

The former inequality is thanks to
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\bm{\alpha}}
achieving
\geq 1
margins on all tokens
[T]-\alpha_{ik}
and the latter arises from the
\delta
-margin violation of
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
at
(i,k)=(1,1)
i.e. Eq. (
101
). Since
\ell
is strictly decreasing with Lipschitz gradient and the scores are upper/lower bounded by an absolute constant (as tokens are bounded,
(h_{k})_{k=1}^{K}
are Lipschitz, and both are fixed), we know that
c_{\texttt{up}}\geq-\ell^{\prime}(\bm{\gamma}_{ik}^{\bm{W}})\geq c_{\texttt{dn}}
for some constants
c_{\texttt{up}}>c_{\texttt{dn}}>0
. Thus, following Eq. (
97
) and the score decomposition (
98
), and using (
102
),(
103
),(
104
) we can write

\displaystyle{\cal{L}}({\bm{\bar{W}}}(R))-{\cal{L}}_{\star}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n}[\ell(\bm{\gamma}_{11}^{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)})-\ell(\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{11})]\geq\frac{c_{\texttt{dn}}}{n}(\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{{11}}-\bm{\gamma}_{11}^{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{\texttt{dn}}}{n}(c_{\bm{\alpha}}P^{11}_{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}-BQ^{11}_{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)})

(105)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{\texttt{dn}}}{n}(1-\bm{s}_{{11}\alpha_{11}}^{R})(0.5c_{\bm{\alpha}}-BTe^{-\varepsilon_{\diamond}\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}\|_{\diamond}})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{\texttt{dn}}}{n}\frac{1}{1+e^{(1-\delta)R\Xi_{\diamond}}}(0.5c_{\bm{\alpha}}-BTe^{-\varepsilon_{\diamond}R_{0}}).

Above, recalling the choice
R_{0}\geq{\cal{O}}(1/\varepsilon_{\diamond})={\cal{O}}(1/\epsilon)
,
R\geq R_{0}
implies
BTe^{-\varepsilon_{\diamond}R_{0}}\leq c_{\bm{\alpha}}/4
to obtain

\displaystyle{\cal{L}}({\bm{\bar{W}}}(R))-{\cal{L}}_{\star}\geq\frac{c_{\texttt{dn}}\cdot c_{\bm{\alpha}}}{4n}\frac{1}{1+e^{(1-\delta)R\Xi_{\diamond}}}.

(106)

Additionally when
{\bm{\alpha}}=\texttt{opt}
, since
Q^{11}_{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}=0
in (
105
), the bound above holds with
R_{0}=0
by directly using (
105
).

Conversely, we upper bound the difference between
{\cal{L}}(\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R})
and
{\cal{L}}_{\star}
as follows. Define the worst-case loss difference for
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
as
(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})=\arg\max_{i\in[n],k\in[K]}[\ell(\bm{\gamma}_{ik}^{\star})-\ell(\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik})]
. Using (
99
)&(
104
), we write

\displaystyle{\cal{L}}(\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R})-{\cal{L}}_{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\in[n],k\in[K]}[\ell(\bm{\gamma}_{ik}^{\star})-\ell(\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{ik})]\leq c_{\texttt{up}}\cdot(\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}-\bm{\gamma}^{\star}_{i^{\prime}k^{\prime}})

(107)

\displaystyle\leq c_{\texttt{up}}\cdot(1-\bm{s}_{i^{\prime}k^{\prime}\alpha_{i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}}^{\star})B

\displaystyle\leq c_{\texttt{up}}\cdot Te^{-R\Xi_{\diamond}}B.

Combining the last inequality and (
106
), we conclude that
{\cal{L}}(\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R})<{\cal{L}}({\bm{\bar{W}}}(R))
whenever

c_{\texttt{up}}T\cdot e^{-R\Xi_{\diamond}}B<\frac{c_{\texttt{dn}}\cdot c_{\bm{\alpha}}}{4n}\frac{1}{1+e^{(1-\delta)R\Xi_{\diamond}}}\iff\frac{e^{R\Xi_{\diamond}}}{1+e^{(1-\delta)R\Xi_{\diamond}}}>\frac{4c_{\texttt{up}}TnB}{c_{\texttt{dn}}c_{\bm{\alpha}}}.

The left hand-side inequality holds for all sufficiently large
R
: Specifically, as soon as
R
obeys
R>\frac{1}{\delta\Xi_{\diamond}}\log(\frac{8c_{\texttt{up}}TnB}{c_{\texttt{dn}}c_{\bm{\alpha}}})
. This completes the proof of the theorem via contradiction as we obtained
{\cal{L}}({\bm{\bar{W}}}(R))>{\cal{L}}(\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R})
.

E.2
Global regularization path

The following result is a direct corollary of Theorem
9
. Namely, we simply restate the final line of this theorem that applies to optimal tokens.

Corollary 1
(Global Convergence of Regularization Path)

Suppose Assumptions
A
&
E
hold and the optimal indices
\texttt{opt}_{ik}=\arg\max_{t\in[T]}\bm{\gamma}_{ikt}
are unique. Consider the global regularization path
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{{\diamond},R}=\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{R}_{m},\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\diamond}\leq R}{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})
. Let
{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\diamond}
be the non-empty solution set of (
SAtt-SVM
) with
{\bm{\alpha}}\leftarrow\texttt{opt}
normalized to have unit
{\diamond}
-norm. Then

\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\texttt{dist}\left(\frac{{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{{\diamond},R}}{R},{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\diamond}\right)

The next corollary directly targets application to (
SERM-W
) and (
SERM-KQ
). This corollary is also a strict generalization of Theorem
2
. Specifically, we immediately recover Theorem
2
by specializing this to the single-output setting
K\leftarrow 1
and full-dimensional parameterization
m\leftarrow d
.

Corollary 2

Suppose Assumptions
A
&
E
hold and the optimal indices
\texttt{opt}_{ik}=\arg\max_{t\in[T]}\bm{\gamma}_{ikt}
are unique. Consider the regularization paths associated to (
SERM-W
) and (
SERM-KQ
):

\displaystyle{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{R}=\underset{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{R}_{m},\|{\bm{W}}\|_{F}\leq R}{\arg\min}{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})\quad\text{and}\quad{\bm{\bar{K}}}_{R},{\bm{\bar{Q}}}_{R}=\underset{\|{{\bm{K}}}\|_{F}^{2}+\|{{\bm{Q}}}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 2R}{\arg\min}{\cal{L}}({\bm{K}},{\bm{Q}})

(108)

Suppose (
SAtt-SVM
) is feasible for
{\bm{\alpha}}\leftarrow\texttt{opt}
. Let
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}
be the unique solution of (
SAtt-SVM
) with Frobenius norm and
{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\star}
be the solution set of (
SAtt-SVM
) with nuclear norm and cost function
\|{{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\star}}\|_{\star}
. We have that

\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\frac{{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{R}}{R}=\frac{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}{\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{F}},\quad\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\texttt{dist}\left(\frac{{\bm{\bar{Q}}}_{R}{\bm{\bar{K}}}_{R}^{\top}}{R},\frac{{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\star}}{\|{{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{\star}}\|_{\star}}\right)=0.

Proof.
We directly apply Corollary
1
with
{\diamond}=F
and
{\diamond}=\star
respectively. To obtain the result on
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{R}
, we note that
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}
is unique because Frobenius norm-squared is strongly convex. To obtain the result on
({\bm{\bar{Q}}}_{R},{\bm{\bar{K}}}_{R})
, we use Lemma
15
and observe that

{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{\star,R}:={\bm{\bar{Q}}}_{R}{\bm{\bar{K}}}_{R}^{\top}\in\underset{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{R}_{m},\|{\bm{W}}\|_{\star}\leq R}{\arg\min}{\cal{L}}(\bm{W}).

We then apply Corollary
1
with
{\diamond}=\star
to conclude with the convergence of the path
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{\star,R}
.

E.2.1
Proof of Theorem
2

Corollary
2
already proves Theorem
2
through the more general Theorem
9
. Below, we provide a self contained proof of Theorem
2
for clarity.

Proof.
Throughout
{\diamond}
denotes either Frobenius norm or nuclear norm. We will prove that
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
asymptotically aligns with the set of globally-optimal directions and also
\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\rightarrow\infty
.
\mathcal{R}_{m}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
denote the manifold of rank
\leq
m
matrices.

Step 1:
Let us first prove that
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
achieves the optimal risk as
R\rightarrow\infty
– rather than problem having finite optima. Define
\Xi_{\diamond}=1/\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{\diamond}
and norm-normalized
\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}=\Xi_{\diamond}\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}
. Note that
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}
separates tokens
opt
from rest of the tokens for each
i\in[n]
. Thus, we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}{\cal{L}}({\bm{\bar{W}}}(R))\leq\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}{\cal{L}}(R\cdot\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}):={\cal{L}}_{\star}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(\bm{\gamma}^{\texttt{opt}}_{i}).

(109)

On the other hand, for any
\bm{W}\in\mathcal{R}_{m}
, define the softmax probabilities
\bm{s}^{(i)}=\mathbb{S}({\bm{X}}_{i}\bm{W}{\bm{z}}_{i})
and attention features
\bm{x}^{\bm{W}}_{i}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{s}^{(i)}_{t}\bm{x}_{t}
. Decompose
\bm{x}^{\bm{W}}_{i}
as

\bm{x}^{\bm{W}}_{i}=\bm{s}^{(i)}_{\texttt{opt}_{i}}\bm{x}_{i\texttt{opt}_{i}}+\sum_{t\neq\texttt{opt}_{i}}\bm{s}^{(i)}_{t}\bm{x}_{it}.
Set
\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{it}=\bm{\gamma}^{\texttt{opt}}_{i}-\bm{\gamma}_{it}=Y_{i}\cdot\bm{v}^{\top}(\bm{x}_{i\texttt{opt}_{i}}-\bm{x}_{it})>0
, and define

\displaystyle B:=\max_{i\in[n]}\max_{t,\tau\in[T]}\|{\bm{v}}\|\cdot\|{\bm{x}_{it}-\bm{x}_{i\tau}}\|\geq\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{it}.

(110)

Define
c_{\texttt{opt}}=\min_{i\in[n],t\neq\texttt{opt}_{i}}\gamma^{\textsl{gap}}_{it}>0
and
\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{i}=Y_{i}\cdot\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{x}^{\bm{W}}_{i}
. We obtain the following score inequalities

\displaystyle\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{i}\leq\bm{\gamma}^{\texttt{opt}}_{i}-c_{\texttt{opt}}(1-\bm{s}^{(i)}_{\texttt{opt}_{i}})<\bm{\gamma}^{\texttt{opt}}_{i},

(111)

\displaystyle|\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{i}-\bm{\gamma}^{\texttt{opt}}_{i}|\leq\|{\bm{v}}\|\cdot\|{\bm{x}^{\bm{W}}_{i}-\bm{x}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{i}}\|\leq\|{\bm{v}}\|\sum_{t\neq\texttt{opt}_{i}}\bm{s}^{(i)}_{t}\|{\bm{x}_{it}-\bm{x}^{\bm{\alpha}}_{i}}\|\leq B(1-\bm{s}^{(i)}_{\texttt{opt}_{i}}).

We will use the
\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{i}-\bm{\gamma}^{\texttt{opt}}_{i}
term in (
111
) to evaluate
\bm{W}
against the reference loss
{\cal{L}}_{\star}
of (
109
). Using the strictly-decreasing nature of
\ell
, we conclude with the fact that for all (finite)
\bm{W}\in\mathcal{R}_{m}
,

{\cal{L}}(\bm{W})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(\bm{\gamma}^{\bm{W}}_{i})>{\cal{L}}_{\star}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(\bm{\gamma}^{\texttt{opt}}_{i}),

which implies
\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\rightarrow\infty
together with (
109
).

Step 2:
To proceed, we show that
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
converges in direction to
{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}
, which denotes the set of SVM minima. Suppose this is not the case and convergence fails. We will obtain a contradiction by showing that
\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R}=R\cdot\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}
achieves a strictly superior loss compared to
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
. Let us introduce the normalized parameters
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)=\frac{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}{R\Xi_{\diamond}}
and
\bm{W}^{\prime}=\frac{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}{\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\Xi_{\diamond}}
. Note that
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)
is obtained by scaling down
\bm{W}^{\prime}
since
\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\leq R
and
\bm{W}^{\prime}
obeys
\|{\bm{W}^{\prime}}\|_{\diamond}=\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{\diamond}
.
Since
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)
fails to converge to
{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}
, for some
\delta>0
, there exists arbitrarily large
R>0
such that
\texttt{dist}\left({\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R),{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}\right)\geq\delta
. This translates to the suboptimality in terms of the margin constraints as follows: First, since nuclear norm dominates Frobenius, distance with respect to the
{\diamond}
-norm obeys
\texttt{dist}_{\diamond}\left({\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R),{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}\right)\geq\delta
. Secondly, using triangle inequality,

\text{this implies that either~~~}\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\leq\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{\diamond}-\delta/2\text{~~~or~~~}\texttt{dist}_{\diamond}\left(\bm{W}^{\prime},{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}\right)\geq\delta/2.

In either scenario,
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)
strictly violates one of the margin constraints of (
SAtt-SVM
): If
\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\leq\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{\diamond}-\delta/2
, then, since the optimal SVM objective is
\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{\diamond}
, there exists a constraint
i,t\neq\texttt{opt}_{i}
for which
\left<(\bm{x}^{\texttt{opt}}_{i}-\bm{x}_{it}){\bm{z}}_{i}^{\top},{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)\right>\leq 1-\frac{\delta}{2\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{\diamond}}
. If
\texttt{dist}_{\diamond}\left(\bm{W}^{\prime},{\cal{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}\right)\geq\delta/2
, then,
\bm{W}^{\prime}
has the same SVM objective but it is strictly bounded away from the solution set. Thus, for some
\epsilon:=\epsilon(\delta)>0
,
\bm{W}^{\prime}
and its scaled down version
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)
strictly violate an SVM constraint achieving margin
\leq 1-\epsilon
. Without losing generality, suppose
{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)
violates the first constraint
i=1
. Thus, for a properly updated
\delta>0
(that is function of the initial
\delta>0
) and for
i=1
and some
support index

\tau\in\mathcal{T}_{1}
,

\displaystyle\left<(\bm{x}^{\texttt{opt}}_{1}-\bm{x}_{1t}){\bm{z}}_{1}^{\top},{\bm{\bar{W}}}_{0}(R)\right>\leq 1-\delta.

(112)

Now, we will argue that this leads to a contradiction by proving
{\cal{L}}(\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R})<{\cal{L}}({\bm{\bar{W}}}(R))
for sufficiently large
R
.

To obtain the result, we establish a refined softmax probability control as in Step 1 by studying distance to
{\cal{L}}_{\star}
. Following (
111
), denote the score function at
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
via
\bm{\gamma}^{R}_{i}:=\bm{\gamma}_{i}^{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}
. Similarly, let
\bm{s}^{R}_{i}=\mathbb{S}({\bm{a}}^{R}_{i})
with
{\bm{a}}^{R}_{i}={\bm{X}}_{i}{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R){\bm{z}}_{i}
. Set the corresponding notation for the reference parameter
\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R}
as
\bm{\gamma}^{\star}_{i},\bm{s}^{\star}_{i},{\bm{a}}^{\star}_{i}
. Recall that
R\geq\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}\|_{\diamond}
and
\Xi_{\diamond}:=1/\|{\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}}\|_{\diamond}
. We note the following softmax inequalities

\displaystyle\bm{s}^{\star}_{i\texttt{opt}_{i}}\geq\frac{1}{1+Te^{-R\Xi_{\diamond}}}\geq 1-Te^{-R\Xi_{\diamond}}\quad\text{for all}\quad i\in[n],

(113)

\displaystyle s^{R}_{i\texttt{opt}_{i}}\leq\frac{1}{1+e^{-(1-\delta)\|{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)}\|_{\diamond}\Xi_{\diamond}}}\leq\frac{1}{1+e^{-(1-\delta)R\Xi_{\diamond}}}\quad\text{for}\quad i=1.

The former inequality is thanks to
\bm{W}^{\textsl{mm}}
achieving
\geq
1
margins on all tokens
[T]-\texttt{opt}_{i}
and the latter arises from the
\delta
-margin violation of
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
at
i=1
i.e. Eq. (
112
). Since
\ell
is strictly decreasing with Lipschitz derivative and the scores are upper/lower bounded by an absolute constant (as tokens are bounded and fixed), we have that
c_{\texttt{up}}\geq-\ell^{\prime}(\bm{\gamma}_{i}^{\bm{W}})\geq c_{\texttt{dn}}
for some constants
c_{\texttt{up}}>c_{\texttt{dn}}>0
. Thus, following Eq. (
110
), the score decomposition (
111
), and (
113
) we can write

\displaystyle{\cal{L}}({\bm{\bar{W}}}(R))-{\cal{L}}_{\star}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n}[\ell(\bm{\gamma}_{1}^{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)})-\ell(\bm{\gamma}^{\texttt{opt}}_{1})]\geq\frac{c_{\texttt{dn}}}{n}(\bm{\gamma}^{\texttt{opt}}_{1}-\bm{\gamma}_{1}^{{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)})

(114)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{\texttt{dn}}}{n}c_{\texttt{opt}}(1-\bm{s}^{R}_{1\texttt{opt}_{1}}).

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{\texttt{dn}}c_{\texttt{opt}}}{n}\frac{1}{1+e^{(1-\delta)R\Xi_{\diamond}}}.

Conversely, we upper bound the difference between
{\cal{L}}(\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R})
and
{\cal{L}}_{\star}
as follows. Define the worst-case loss difference for
{\bm{\bar{W}}}(R)
as
j=\arg\max_{i\in[n]}[\ell(\bm{\gamma}_{i}^{\star})-\ell(\bm{\gamma}^{\texttt{opt}}_{i})]
. Using (
111
)&(
113
), we write

\displaystyle{\cal{L}}(\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R})-{\cal{L}}_{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\in[n]}[\ell(\bm{\gamma}_{i}^{\star})-\ell(\bm{\gamma}^{\texttt{opt}}_{i})]\leq c_{\texttt{up}}\cdot(\bm{\gamma}^{\texttt{opt}}_{j}-\bm{\gamma}^{\star}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq c_{\texttt{up}}\cdot(1-\bm{s}^{\star}_{j\texttt{opt}_{j}})B

\displaystyle\leq c_{\texttt{up}}\cdot Te^{-R\Xi_{\diamond}}B.

Combining the last inequality and (
114
), we conclude that
{\cal{L}}(\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R})<{\cal{L}}({\bm{\bar{W}}}(R))
whenever

c_{\texttt{up}}T\cdot e^{-R\Xi_{\diamond}}B<\frac{c_{\texttt{dn}}\cdot c_{\texttt{opt}}}{n}\frac{1}{1+e^{(1-\delta)R\Xi_{\diamond}}}\iff\frac{e^{R\Xi_{\diamond}}}{1+e^{(1-\delta)R\Xi_{\diamond}}}>\frac{c_{\texttt{up}}TnB}{c_{\texttt{dn}}c_{\texttt{opt}}}.

The left hand-side inequality holds for all sufficiently large
R
: Specifically, as soon as
R
obeys
R>\frac{1}{\delta\Xi_{\diamond}}\log(\frac{2c_{\texttt{up}}TnB}{c_{\texttt{dn}}c_{\texttt{opt}}})
. This completes the proof of the theorem by contradiction since we obtained
{\cal{L}}({\bm{\bar{W}}}(R))>{\cal{L}}(\bar{\bm{W}}^{\textsl{mm}}_{R})
.
